# Aegis PMA brief · RERUN r2 (not live) · for Tue 3 Nov 2026 (US open 22:30 SGT) · AQE data to COB Mon 2 Nov

Narrative only: every verdict, status and trigger below is read from consensus.json and decision.json; nothing here changes a call. RERUN r2 on the new code released today, AQE export of 14:51:32 SGT, 48 names in the room.

**Declared degradations and rerun findings (each one is in Read with care, at the end of the body):**
- RERUN r2 on the new code, not live: data fetched by code (hourly bars native from FMP; fundamentals and closes by the AQE run), with no AI data couriers. AQE export 14:51:32 SGT carrying the Mon 2 Nov close, 350 rows, 2 held (live), 0 data-quality flags.
- Declared in the run notes: the code-built closes file was incomplete (353 tickers vs 530) and cut graded advances from 95 to 51, so the scorecard is graded on the original 3 Nov run's verified closes; the code-built copy is kept as closes_scorecard.codebuilt.json and the defect is reported to engineering.
- Slate cap: the cap is 15 but 20 names survived, so TTEK, SWK, GEN, FTV and EW (all WATCH) were cut by a tie-break on the order in decision.json and never ratified, though decision.json itself counts 13 watch and 0 not slated.
- QA note: the AJG hold could not change the ticket's trigger, so AJG dropped on the same words it was held for; some quotes in the slate's agreed-reasons were attributed to a decider who had not backed that name (AJG, PFE, USFD, VRTX); and the coverage report under-reports fields several deciders called unserved.
- Rationale: 21 of 22 rationale amendments were NOT_MERGED because the clause and size caps were full; they appear as minority notes where the code attached them.
- Committee conviction is 3 of 5 on 13 of 14 slated names (CHD alone is 4), so it orders almost nothing today.
- Funnel measurement: 145 claims routed, 290 rulings (92 confirm, 171 refute, 27 could not see); 20 names survived tier 1, 24 were dropped and 4 had no backing at the blind vote; stances changed on a peer's evidence: 3 (NWL, UPS, VVV), all on names that dropped.
- Crown file DEGRADED: its cross-checks (DSPX and implied correlation) disagree with the single-stock vs index volatility gap; oldest source 2 Nov (1 day behind), positioning to 27 Oct.
- Inbox clean not produced: the run manifest declares inbox_clean.json absent.
- Book and stops: broker stops reused from the original 3 Nov run (journal 2 Nov; MP 36.66, V 391.80); no new broker read (US closed).
- Ledgers: the verdict ledger is the 2 Nov run's and the phase4 ledger the 29 Oct run's (same as the original 3 Nov run), so repeat counts come from that base.
- Fundamentals: 347 of 351 served, 4 unserved; brackets are render-only levels for 13 names and none is sized (no dynCap in the committee run).
- Market kill: the only one filed (index distribution days reaching 4-5 inside 4-5 weeks) has no symbol or level, so code cannot watch it.

## §0A EXECUTIVE CONSENSUS — WHY THESE ADVANCE

**The book in three lines:**
- What they share: seven defensive leaders at or within 15.14% of a 52-week high (PYPL is the farthest, five sit at the high), four of them Consumer Staples, six with zero or negative 30-day beta, and all bought on an hourly close over yesterday's high, never at market.
- Agreement: carried over a real objection on all seven; the objections are about the entry, not the stock (two ask for a pullback first on WTRG and KDP, five ask for a 1.40-times volume test or a group-rank test on PYPL, USFD, CHD, KO and PFE), and the strongest agreement is CHD, the one name the committee rates 4 of 5.
- What invalidates the group thesis: SPY losing the 750 put wall in a RISK OFF, short-gamma tape, which would send these breakouts back under their pivots together.

**The QA seat's process read (it held one ticket, AJG, and found the slate does not say exactly what the deciders said):**
- Evidence was read: every ruling cites grid values; the funnel filtered (290 rulings, 171 refute, 24 names dropped) but only 3 stances changed, all on names that dropped anyway, no survivor's stance moved, and only one decider conceded on evidence (KO).
- The slate omits five survivors (FTV, EW, TTEK, SWK, GEN, all watch names) that were never ratified, although decision.json counts 13 watch and 0 not slated.
- The slate's agreed-reasons quote a decider as backing AJG, PFE, USFD and VRTX when that decider had not backed those names; the dissent list carries the losing timing camp on WTRG and KDP but not on the watch tickets.
- One decider objected at ratification to the very trigger it had filed as act-now earlier on PYPL, USFD, CHD, KO and PFE; those objections are what make all five CONTESTED, and which stance stands was never asked.
- One trigger word (an hourly close above yesterday's high) sits on 13 of the 15 slate tickets; the 1.40-times volume filter lives only in the plain words on WTRG, KDP, DLTR and VLTO, and the request to add it on CHD, PYPL, KO and MA went unanswered.
- Triggers on AJG, FTV, VRTX, MA, TTEK, SWK and GEN are one-seat tie-breaks, not the modal trigger; the AJG hold asked whether the room would ratify a watch on an hourly reclaim of the 20-day, but the hold could not change the ticket's trigger, so AJG dropped on the same words.
- The coverage report under-reports fields several deciders called unserved (fill difficulty, ADX, stochastics, bar-range history among them); no bracket reason was found anywhere.

### WTRG — ADVANCE · conviction 3

A leader at its high on every frame: structure 86.3, 1.83 times its average volume, up 12.34% over 63 sessions while the index fell 2.49%. Carried over a real objection: it is bought 3.66 daily ranges above its 20-day average with no pullback yet, a chase rather than a test, and the objection clears only if the ticket waits for the first pullback into the rising 20-day average (41.07). The buy line already sits past two methods' too-late line (44.64), so it must trigger cleanly and hold a daily close above 42.51.

### KDP — ADVANCE · conviction 3

New high on every frame, momentum score 97 and accelerating, beating the market. Carried over a real objection: the hourly closed at 99% of the day's range 3.55 daily ranges above its 20-day average, a possible climax bar, and the objection clears only if the ticket waits for a first pullback toward the rising 21-day average before the hourly trigger counts. It has drifted +2.73% since its 2 Nov advance (1.2x its own risk), so yesterday's entry is gone, and it must hold a daily close above 35.40.

### PYPL — ADVANCE · conviction 3

Price is at the test, 1.43% past the pivot and only 1.65 daily ranges above its 20-day average, the least stretched ADVANCE, with an AQE flow score of 86.8. Carried over a real objection: the break came on 1.30 times average volume, under the 1.40 bar one method treats as a hard rejection, from AQE rank 135 and 15.14% under its 52-week high; it clears only if the trigger adds volume 1.4x normal or better (a second objection asks for a retest of value at 57.26 instead). It must hold a daily close above 57.27.

### USFD — ADVANCE · conviction 3

Institutions showed up on the wide bar (1.82 times volume) and price sits at the test of the broken level, 2.0 daily ranges above its 20-day average. Carried over a real objection: it is the sympathy name, AQE rank 114 in Staples behind KDP and CHD, and it clears only if USFD ranks top three in its group (otherwise take KDP, the leader); a second objection asks for a pullback into the rising 20-day average. It must hold a daily close above 104.34, and it is the fourth Staples name on the list.

### CHD — ADVANCE · conviction 4

The No.2 Staples name (AQE rank 36) breaking at the high with a momentum score of 100, and the one ADVANCE the committee rates 4 of 5. Carried over a real objection: the break printed 1.34 times average volume, under the 1.40 bar one method treats as a hard rejection, and it clears only if the trigger adds volume 1.4x normal or better (a second objection asks for a pullback to the broken level instead). It must hold a daily close above 108.93.

### KO — ADVANCE · conviction 3

The least extended Staples break: 0.48% past its pivot and 2.55 daily ranges above its 20-day average, beating the market. Carried over a real objection: it broke on 1.36 times average volume, under the 1.40 bar, from AQE rank 221, far outside its group's top three, and it clears only if the trigger adds volume 1.4x normal or better (a second objection asks for a pullback into the rising 20-day average). Its AQE flow score of 48.7 is the weakest on the list, and it must hold a daily close above 95.37.

### PFE — ADVANCE · conviction 3

A leader at the pivot, not a chase (2.27 daily ranges above its 20-day average), with force building and an AQE flow score of 78.9, advanced for the second straight session at the same entry. Carried over a real objection: it is a laggard, AQE rank 109 in Healthcare, and the new high carries a bearish divergence on two indicators, a distribution caution; it clears only if PFE ranks top three in Healthcare (a second objection asks for a retest of the 30.44 pivot instead). Earnings are shrinking (EPS -4.2% last year), so this is a chart trade that lives or dies on a daily close above 30.44.

## §0B LANES

WTRG and USFD are PULLBACK-lane names; the other five ADVANCE names and all seven watch names are MOMENTUM-lane. Under today's two-tier funnel every survivor was decided by the whole room, so the lane is a reading label today, not a different set of deciders.

### Part 1c · the AJG hold round (forms/floor2, verbatim)

- elder-lens: Yes, I ratify AJG as WATCH on reclaim:ma_20 (H1). I filed no objection on AJG; I agreed in_zone:ma_20 because elder_5d [7, 7, 7, 10, 10] is a summer plateau and Elder buys the pullback into a rising average (C10, C20). The reclaim keeps that order: the pullback into the 20-day prints first, then the hourly turn back over it is the timing bar. Weekly tide still up, W ema9 258.48 > ema21 249.07 (C21). My stance is unchanged. What voids it for me: elder <= 1 at the pullback close (red, buying forbidden, C3), or a daily close below ma_50.
- livermore: HOLD. reclaim:ma_20 (H1) is a turn up, but it fires still inside the reaction, under the prior high. C5: never buy the pullback, buy the new high that proves the pullback is over; C19: a return toward the pivotal point is not enough. AJG sits 0.9% under its W and D 20-bar highs, so a new-high trigger is close, not a chase. I keep SUPPORT on the name and my OBJECT on the trigger. One observable clears it: the ticket trigger reads close_above:pivot_high (271.27) or h1_close_above:bar_high, a fresh high. Then I ratify WATCH. No bracket reason (R1).
- minervini: Yes, I would ratify AJG as WATCH on raschke's reclaim:ma_20 (H1). My OBJECT was to in_zone:ma_20 buying the anticipated low; my would_clear asked for the turn back up out of the ma_20 zone. An hourly close back above the 20-day after a pullback is that turn (C9: add on the turn up, never the low; C5). No mind changed: my stance on the name stays SUPPORT on the template (entry 278.89 > ma_50 248.67 > ma_100 246.32 > ma_200 232.17, rs_rank_pct 86.61). I am wrong on a daily close below pivot_high 271.27 or ma_50 248.67.
- oneil: HOLD. reclaim:ma_20 is better than a resting buy in the zone: it waits for the turn up. But it is still a retracement entry, not a pivot breakout, and it carries no volume test. C16 says a breakout under +40% above average is a hard rejection; C17 says buy at the pivot. Leadership passes (No.1 Financials, rank 15, rs LEADER) and day_vol 1.79 passed on the close, but lens coil '--' leaves the base uncertified (R3), so I stay ABSTAIN. One thing changes my mind: the trigger reads an hourly close above bar_high on day_vol 1.40 or better.
- raschke: Yes, I ratify AJG as WATCH on reclaim:ma_20 (H1), my tier2 trigger; stance unchanged, SUPPORT/WAIT. A RETRACEMENT (C2, C7 shape, ADX not served): W and D HH/HL 0.9% under the highs, D close 278.89 over ema21 256.8862, H1 red at 71% of range. A resting buy inside the ma_20 zone buys a pullback that has not turned; my canon wants the turn confirmed, an hourly close back above the 20-day. Wrong if a daily close is more than 1 ATR under the 20-day after the pullback. Would change my mind: a range-expansion leg off the high before any pullback prints (C19), which makes it climax, not retracement.
- seow: I support AJG and hold my objection. An H1 reclaim of ma_20 beats a resting buy in the zone, but is not my entry. AJG is a position case: a new high, ABOVE_STRUCTURE, and the entry is a buy stop over bar_high (C27). A pullback to the 20-day makes it a swing case: that needs a daily set-up bar with all five conditions, entry a buy stop over its high (C3, C5). My canon does not trade hourly closes. What would change my mind: a daily bar showing cci_20 under -100, bar_low at or under ma_20, and close over ma_40. Then I ratify WATCH with the trigger trade_above:bar_high on D.
- wyckoff: Yes, I ratify AJG as WATCH on reclaim:ma_20 (H1). My OBJECT was to in_zone:ma_20 'then buy there': a resting buy that bets on two moves when one can be projected (C12). reclaim:ma_20 asks the market to pull back into the zone and then close back above it on the hourly: follow-through is required before entry, which is what my would_clear asked for. The 20-day pullback is an allowed trend entry (C7, C3). Still SUPPORT. Wrong if: close back under structure_shift_ref by half an ATR, or a bearish change of character.
